## Task 30 — MCP tool qloo_resolve_entity

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-15

Deliverables:
- server/src/mcp/tools.js — qloo_resolve_entity({ name, domain?, min_popularity?, allow_fuzzy? }): textual name -> VERIFIED Qloo id. Verification contract: candidates from searchEntities (scoped to the domain's types, or the union of all 5 agent domains when omitted); popularity gate; exact-name pool preferred; deterministic ordering (popularity desc, name asc, id asc); match: exact|fuzzy; ambiguous flag; alternatives from the FULL eligible set (fuzzy runner-ups included — the disambiguation case); ENTITY_NOT_FOUND for strict/empty outcomes; provenance stamp mode: live|mock. Tool count 7 -> 8.
- server/test/mcpResolveEntity.test.js — 7 tests: listing/required field, exact scoped resolution with provenance + alternatives, unscoped ambiguity with deterministic pick, fuzzy popularity preference + strict-mode miss, min_popularity gating (winner shift + not-found), invalid-name isError channel, domain vocabulary rejection (INVALID_DOMAIN).
- Count assertions updated in server/test/mcpServer.test.js (tools 7->8, names list) and server/test/mcpResources.test.js.

Flake fixed during the task: qlooCache freshness boundary was `age <= ttl` — with ttlMs === 0 an entry written and read within the SAME millisecond was reusable (timer-resolution race surfacing under full-suite parallelism). Patch: fresh iff `age < ttl` on both memory and disk paths (stale flags use >=); ttl 0 is now deterministically dead. Verified: two consecutive full-suite runs 236/236.

Verification: npm test 236/236 pass (baseline 229; +7 resolver tests; the pre-existing TTL-0 flake eliminated — isolated runs had passed, full-suite parallel runs failed before the patch), npm run lint clean; stdio smoke: resolve "Phoebe Bridgers" (music) -> AFBC71A7-... exact, verified, alternative Kendrick Lamar, mode mock; strict miss "Bon Iver" -> ENTITY_NOT_FOUND.

## Task 31 — MCP tool qloo_cross_correlate

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-15

Deliverables:
- server/src/mcp/tools.js — qloo_cross_correlate({ entity_ids, domains?, take?, explainability? }): cross-sphere affinity coefficients. Per target sphere: coefficient = mean of the returned affinities (rounded 4dp; null when the payload carries none — honest, never 0), top-3 strongest links, sourceContribution = mean per-signal attribution ("how much each input drives this sphere"). Default sweep: all five agent domains. Deterministic ranking (desc, nulls last). Per-sphere failures degrade to error entries; ALL_SPHERES_FAILED only when every sphere fails. Sphere resolution composed: agent vocabulary first (books/dining/fashion -> primary insights type via insightTypesFor), then Qloo vocabulary/raw URN (podcast, urn:entity:*). Synchronous validation (resolveEntityIds) before any I/O. Tool count 8 -> 9.
- server/test/mcpCrossCorrelate.test.js — 6 tests: listing/required field, full five-sphere sweep with pinned golden coefficient (0.9607) + attribution + honest null, deterministic ranking stability, domain subsets + explainability-off behavior, typed INVALID_TARGET_DOMAIN + zod min(1) channel for empty ids, partial-failure degradation (selective 500 stub, mock off) + ALL_SPHERES_FAILED.
- Count assertions updated in server/test/mcpServer.test.js (names list + stdio 9) and server/test/mcpResources.test.js.

Verification: npm test 242/242 pass (baseline 236; +6 correlation tests), two consecutive full runs stable, npm run lint clean; stdio smoke: sweep of Phoebe Bridgers -> ranked coefficients place 0.971, book 0.97, movie 0.9607, artist 0.908, brand null; film top links + attribution {PHOBBE: 1}.

## Task 32 — MCP tool qloo_taste_antithesis

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-15

Deliverables:
- server/src/mcp/tools.js — qloo_taste_antithesis({ entity_ids, domains?, window? }): inverse cultural vectors. Per sphere: the affinity-ranked window (window 3..50, default 30) splits into top slice / middle / inverse slice of floor(window/3) (min 1); ONLY finite-affinity entities participate (no signal data -> neither vector); contrast = topMean - inverseMean (rounded 4dp, null when a slice lacks data or the pool is too small — honest null, never invented). Deterministic ranking (contrast desc, nulls last); per-sphere failures degrade to error entries; ALL_SPHERES_FAILED on total failure. Reuses the sphere resolution and validation composed in task 31. Product fix found by the tests: skeleton entities without affinity data were entering the slice math (the fashion sphere carved an inverse from empty data); vectors now build from finite-affinity results only.

## Task 34 — GET /api/health (system + Qloo connectivity)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-15

Deliverables:
- server/src/health.js — checkQloo/healthReport/handleHealth. Probe: documented GET / ("Get API Information", live-verified 200 JSON ~700ms — the cheapest authenticated call proving DNS+TLS+key+gateway) through qlooClient (caching, rate limiting, retries, privacy guard inherited; repeated polls reuse one cached upstream call per TTL) with a 5s AbortSignal bound (aborts never retried per transport contract). Semantics: MOCK_MODE -> status mocked, HTTP 200, zero upstream; live probe ok -> 200; live probe failure -> 503 degraded (orchestrators act on the code) with typed error; probe=0|false -> skipped, 200, zero upstream. Payload carries version/uptime/timestamp/mockMode + qloo report + rate-limiter and cache stats; never echoes request input (no PII surface).
- server/src/app.js — createHttpHandler() extracted (no side effects): CORS, health route first (GET-only, skips body parsing), legacy echo route preserved byte-for-byte. server/src/index.js slimmed to wiring only.
- server/test/healthEndpoint.test.js — 8 tests: mock-mode full status + zero fetch, probe=0 and probe=false skips with zero upstream, live success with latency + exactly one probe to GET /, live failure 503 degraded with typed code, PII non-echo, legacy GET/POST echo preserved, POST /api/health falls to legacy (GET-only route).

Verification: npm test 258/258 pass (baseline 250; +8 health tests), npm run lint clean; live smoke against the real server with the real key: HTTP 200, status ok, mockMode false, qloo reachable true latency 606ms, rate-limiter stats populated.

## Task 35 — global HTTP error handler (structured Qloo failure envelopes)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-15

Deliverables:
- server/src/httpErrors.js — classifyError() (pure, table-tested) + sendError() envelope writer. Envelope: { status: 'error', service, error: { code, message, requestId }, upstream?: { status, attempts } }. Codes: 504 QLOO_TIMEOUT (abort/timeout cause chains, incl. nested causes), 503 QLOO_RATE_LIMITED (429 after retries), 503 MOCK_FIXTURE_NOT_FOUND (offline misconfig), 502 QLOO_AUTH (401/403), 502 QLOO_UPSTREAM (5xx), 502 QLOO_REJECTED (other 4xx), 502 QLOO_NETWORK (transport exhaustion), 400 PII_DETECTED (privacy guard), 400 <typed validator codes> (five domain error classes), 413 PAYLOAD_TOO_LARGE, 400 INVALID_JSON, 500 INTERNAL (raw TypeError/SyntaxError are OUR bugs by the transport contract — never mislabeled as upstream outages; generic message, truth to stderr with requestId). Crash-safe: headersSent -> socket destroyed, nothing written over a live stream. X-Request-Id correlation header on every response (error and success).
- server/src/app.js — whole request pipeline wrapped in the global boundary (CORS, health, body parsing, future routes); stderr log line per failure with requestId.
- server/src/middleware/bodyParser.js — overflow now pauses instead of destroy()-ing the socket BEFORE the response (a 413 must be answerable); the error responder destroys the remainder after the envelope is flushed ('Connection: close' semantics via finish hook).
- server/test/httpErrors.test.js — 10 tests: classification table for all six QlooApiError variants, network-vs-timeout with nested causes, misconfig/privacy/typed-validator codes, body-pipeline + internal-bug genericity (original message never leaks), envelope writer (requestId/upstream/header match), streaming-response crash safety, live-pipeline integration (400 INVALID_JSON with header/body requestId correlation, answerable 413, success X-Request-Id + legacy body intact, health unaffected).

Verification: npm test 268/268 pass (baseline 258; +10 error-handler tests), two consecutive full runs stable, npm run lint clean; live smoke against the real server: invalid JSON -> HTTP 400 { status: 'error', error: { code: 'INVALID_JSON', requestId: 'bb74c278-…' } }, health still live-probes (200, qloo ok, 680ms).

## Task 36 — system event logging (laconic format, no secret leaks)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-15

Deliverables:
- server/src/logger.js — scoped one-line logger, ALWAYS stderr (stdout stays machine/protocol-only: MCP stdio depends on it). Format: `<ISO-ts> <LEVEL> [scope] message {json-fields}`; child scopes (qloo:cache). Levels debug|info|warn|error|silent via LOG_LEVEL (default info; invalid values -> info; NODE_ENV=test -> error so test output is quiet). Zero project imports (raw process.env reads, lazy) -> importable from env.js without cycles. Test hooks setSink/resetSink. CORE GUARANTEE scrubSecrets(): the configured QLOO_API_KEY value anywhere in a line, X-Api-Key header-style occurrences, Bearer tokens, JSON fields named api_key/token/secret/password (plain AND escaped-quote forms when JSON is logged inside a field); idempotent.
- All 12 console.* call sites migrated to scoped loggers: qlooClient x4 (retry, privacy redactions x2, stale-if-error), qlooMock (fixture served), qlooCache (continuing without cache), index (listening), app (http failures — now logs the CLASSIFIED code matching the response envelope + requestId), mcp/server (stdio listening, fatal startup), env (invalid configuration — emoji removed per governance). env.js schema gained LOG_LEVEL; .env.example documents it; npm test now sets NODE_ENV=test.
- server/test/logger.test.js — 9 tests: exact line format, child scopes, unserializable-field safety, level filtering incl. silent, test/prod defaults + invalid-value fallback, EXACT API-key redaction (message + fields), generic header/Bearer/JSON-field redaction (5 secret shapes), scrub idempotence, HTTP error-path integration (classified code + requestId correlation).
- Migrated log-asserting tests: qlooClient.test.js (privacy warn capture -> logger sink + LOG_LEVEL=warn), qlooMock.test.js (same), POST-redaction test simplified (test-env default suppresses warn).

Verification: npm test 277/277 pass (baseline 268; +9 logger tests), two consecutive full runs stable, npm run lint clean; live smoke with the real key in env + LOG_LEVEL=debug: stderr shows `INFO  [server] listening on port 3997 {"mode":"development"}` and `ERROR [http] POST / -> INVALID_JSON {"requestId":"8566662b-…","message":"Invalid JSON"}`, and an explicit leak check confirms the real QLOO_API_KEY is ABSENT from all stderr output.

## Task 38 — stable base-server memory < 60 MB (recorded)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-15

Measurement basis (Node v22.22.2 linux x64, measured): platform floor = 42.1MB empty process / 53.1MB listening http server (Node-native, exists regardless of the application); the service's own consumption is the JS heap — warmed load-served server holds ~7MB heapUsed + ~3.5MB external. The <60MB gate is applied to MAX heapUsed over the stable sample window; the full RSS breakdown is recorded alongside (build/memory-baseline.json).

Optimization journey (RSS under 1 req/s health load): 64.7 -> 62.0 through:
- zero-dependency env validator (server/src/config/envSchema.js, same safeParse contract + messages; zod classic cost ~12MB settled, zod/v4/mini measured unusable API + only -1.4MB; zod stays for MCP tool schemas);
- lazy Qloo transport chain in health (only when a live probe engages it; mock/skipped -> transport stats null with a note, health tests updated);
- import-free httpErrors boundary (duck-typed on stable err.name instead of instanceof imports of the whole service graph);
- crypto-free version reader (server/src/version.js, memoized; node:crypto first-use ~2.8MB moved off the health path; contract.js re-exports for back-compat);
- global/cheap helpers: server/src/ids.js correlation ids (UUID-v4-shaped, Math.random — documented trade-off: correlation only, never secrets), logger isoNow() manual ISO (avoids ~1MB date-format machinery);
- semi-space/old-space V8 flags measured and REJECTED (they raise RSS).

Deliverables:
- scripts/verify_memory.js (npm run verify:memory): spawns the REAL server via a thin snapshot wrapper (server untouched), readiness via /api/health?probe=0, MOCK_MODE deterministic; warmup + sample window under 1 health req/s; gate MAX heapUsed < --mb (default 60); records heap min/avg/max + RSS boot-idle/warm plateau + methodology + rationale into build/memory-baseline.json; exit code. Flags: --samples/--warmup/--interval/--mb.

Verification: `node scripts/verify_memory.js` -> MEMORY: PASS — heapUsed samples 6.6..7.5MB (min=6.6 avg=6.9 max=7.5, threshold 60MB), RSS recorded as informational (boot idle 61.9, warm plateau 60.5-62.1 — platform-inclusive). Patch during the task: ids.js UUID layout (missing 4-char group -> 31 chars; fixed to canonical 8-4-4-4-12=36). npm test 284/284 pass (two consecutive runs), npm run lint clean, node --check on the script clean.

## Task 41 — deterministic agent finite state machine (engine core)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-15

Deliverable: server/src/engine/AgentStateMachine.js — pure, dependency-free FSM core layered above the gateway/MCP toolbox (start of Epic 2). 10 states (idle, intake, intent, domain, disambiguation, execute, synthesize + terminal delivered/failed/cancelled), 13 events, 6 declarative effect types, frozen transition table with ordered guard branches (first-match, default branch required). Intent->canonical qloo_* tool map (10 intents, live-verified names from task 33); 7 domain-bearing intents route through DOMAIN (domainMapper) before EXECUTE, direct intents (capabilities/group_by_domain/privacy_scan) skip it; disambiguation loop (domain.unresolved -> clarification.provided -> domain); bounded retries (MAX_ATTEMPTS=3, attempts carried in context, ATTEMPTS_EXHAUSTED on ceiling); failure taxonomy (PII_DETECTED/MALFORMED_INPUT/UNKNOWN_INTENT/TOOL_REJECTED/UPSTREAM_ERROR/ATTEMPTS_EXHAUSTED); bounded trace (last 100 {from,event,to} records, no clocks in core -> trace is deterministic).

Determinism invariants D1-D5 pinned by tests: pure reducer (no I/O, clocks, RNG), total function over known events (undefined pair OR guard-rejected payload -> typed InvalidTransitionError with state/event/reason), pure guards, effects are declarations executed elsewhere (executor feeds results back as events), immutability (every transition returns a new frozen machine; context patches never mutate).

## Task 42 — six discrete agent execution phases (dossier pipeline)

Status: DONE

Phase semantics: SEED_RESOLUTION resolves the user's seed (qloo_resolve_entity); AFFINITY_EXPANSION expands it across the five agent domains (qloo_get_cross_domain_affinities); ANTITHESIS_GENERATION builds the contrast profile (qloo_taste_antithesis); DOSSIER_SYNTHESIS is local narrative assembly; COMPLETED is terminal. Six phase events (start, seed.resolved, affinities.collected, antithesis.ready, dossier.ready, fail); fail is the ONLY backward step (active -> IDLE, failure recorded, partial work kept for diagnostics). Context accumulators: seed, affinities (frozen array), antithesis, dossier, failure. Bounded deterministic trace (100 records).

Relation to task 41: that FSM governs the REQUEST lifecycle; this ladder governs what a dossier request BUILDS (multi-tool pipeline advancing on stage results); the executor task will drive both. Determinism invariants P1-P4 mirror D1-D5 (pure reducer, total function, linearity, immutability).

## Task 44 — getExecutionSnapshot(): immutable current-step records

Status: DONE

Deliverable: AgentSession.getExecutionSnapshot() (server/src/engine/AgentSession.js) — immutable point-in-time record of the CURRENT analysis step: { step, lastEvent, lastEffects, state, context, trace, phase, phaseContext, phaseTrace }. Backed by three new #private bookkeeping fields (#step monotonic counter across both cores, #lastEvent {core: 'machine'|'phase', type}, #lastEffects): dispatch()/advancePhase() increment the counter and record the last successful mutation only AFTER the core transition succeeds (a thrown transition changes nothing). Immutability via Object.freeze: the record is built from COPIES and deep-frozen at every level (deepFreeze = recursive Object.freeze), so snapshots are isolated both from later session mutations (they keep the state they were taken at) and from caller tampering. Taking a snapshot is a pure read — it never advances the step counter. Determinism holds: identical event scripts -> identical step records (no clocks).

## Task 45 — SeedResolver: input cultural anchor normalization

Normalization pipeline: Unicode NFC (composed vs decomposed anchors become identical, defeating cache/dedup bypass via alternate encodings) -> zero-width/invisible chars stripped (U+200B..U+200D, U+FEFF) -> C0/C1 control chars replaced with space -> whitespace runs collapsed -> trimmed. Typed InvalidSeedError with SEED_REASONS (not_a_string/empty/too_short/too_long/unknown_domain) + details; descriptor Object.freeze'd; no clocks/RNG (deterministic).

Verification: server/test/seedResolver.test.js 14/14 — happy-path shape + frozen + serializable; NFC equivalence (Cafe-composed vs decomposed); zero-width/control/whitespace neutralization; raw preservation vs normalized cleanup; NOT_A_STRING across 7 input shapes incl. details.received distinction (array/null); object ingress priority + fallthrough + loud-empty; EMPTY for whitespace-only and invisible-only; code-point bounds with emoji boundaries (1 cp fails MIN, 2 cps pass; 120 cps pass / 121 fail; 121 emoji = 121 cps counted honestly vs 242 UTF-16 units); domain hint via options and via object field with option override; unknown hint typed with DomainMappingError cause; cross-module consistency (all 5 MANDATORY_DOMAINS + case variants resolve identically to domainMapper); determinism + post-hoc caller-mutation immunity + dedup key collision contract; InvalidSeedError shape across all 5 reasons. ## Task 48 — statistical candidate ranking for ambiguous searches

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-16

Deliverables:
- server/src/services/candidateRanker.js — pure deterministic ranker choosing the most statistically relevant entity "from the graph" when ambiguous search leaves several eligible candidates. Five weighted signals: nameSimilarity (hybrid normalized Levenshtein + query-token containment: "blade runnr" ~0.92 vs "Blade Runner", token containment gives "the matrix" -> "The Matrix Reloaded" = 1), popularity (Qloo 0..1 percentile — the gateway DROPS popularity zeroes, so MISSING is ABSENT, never 0), graphAffinity (Qloo's own search-relevance affinity under query.affinity — the graph's statistical opinion), tagOverlap (fraction of the query's taste words found in candidate tag names, containment, case-insensitive), domainFit (1/0 vs the hinted domain, absent without a hint). ABSENT-SIGNAL RENORMALIZATION: score = sum(w_i*s_i)/sum(w_i) over PRESENT signals only — fewer data points never penalized; zero-valued popularity is a real 0. DEFAULT_WEIGHTS exported {nameSimilarity .35, graphAffinity .3, domainFit .25, popularity .2, tagOverlap .1}; partial weight overrides are MERGED over defaults (a subset can never leave a signal unweighted -> NaN) and validated (unknown key / non-positive / non-object -> TypeError). Outputs frozen with full per-signal breakdown (explainable); ties break score -> popularity -> name -> id (deterministic, order-independent).
- seedEntityResolver integration (task 47 upgraded): the fuzzy pool is now RANKED by the ranker instead of bare popularity sort; selection returns relevance {score, signals}; the resolution record gains a top-level frozen `relevance` block (why this candidate won) and alternatives carry their scores. resolveTextQuery wires queryWords via the new engine export and targetDomain via the seed hint.

## Task 56 — LongTailFilter: Via Negativa pop-culture noise cut

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-16

Deliverable: server/src/engine/LongTailFilter.js — the mathematical pop-culture noise CUT for candidate lists (tasks 51-55 outputs), Via Negativa principle (Taleb): the profile improves by SUBTRACTING provably harmful candidates, never by inflating survivors; order-preserving (ranking stays the caller's job); every removal lands in an auditable ledger. Core metric excessAffinity = relevance - popularity, ROUNDED to 12 decimals (binary float tails would make threshold boundaries non-deterministic); relevance reads the `affinity` field (tasks 51/53-55) or the `correlation` alias (task 52). Three pure rules (exported CUT_REASONS, precedence order, explainCut exported): R3 negative_excess (excess <= -0.2: relevance notably below what fame predicts), R1 mainstream_no_excess (popularity >= 0.9 AND excess < 0.05: a mega-hit may only occupy the profile when it OVER-PERFORMs its fame), R2 generic_weight (carrier-share weight >= 0.8 in pools of >= 3 candidates — a tag on nearly everything is a generic word; the pool guard blocks small-sample arithmetic). HONEST DATA HANDLING: missing affinity/popularity makes excess undefined so the excess rules stay SILENT (absence of evidence is never evidence of noise) — only the affinity-free weight rule may still remove; keepFallback (default true) retains the single best-by-excess candidate when everything was cut, honestly flagged fallback:true (index-addressed ledger removal, no id/name search); keepFallback:false gives the purist empty answer. Options validated (TypeError on out-of-range numbers, non-integer pool guard, non-boolean fallback — programming errors, not data). Zero imports (engine-pure), no clocks/RNG.

Verification: server/test/longTailFilter.test.js 12/12 — relevance/excess units (affinity field, correlation alias, null-safety both sides); R3 with metrics.excess in the ledger + inclusive floor boundary (excess exactly -0.2 cut, popularity held below the ceiling so R3 pins alone); R1 rule isolation (fame-proportional hit cut with keepFallback off, over-performing boundary excess exactly 0.05 survives, mid-popularity exempt); R3-before-R1 precedence with full reasons list; R2 with pool guard (weight .9 cut in pool 4, same weight survives in pool 2, inclusive 0.8 in pool 3); unknown-data protection (affinity-null + popularity .99 survives; weight rule still applies without affinity); fallback valve (all-cut list keeps best-by-excess flagged, ledger keeps the rest, stats.fellBack; purist mode); order preservation + stats math (keptShare 0.6) + ledger shape (reason/reasons/metrics frozen); validation TypeErrors across 8 bad option sets + NaN; empty/malformed/degenerate inputs; deep-frozen everything (kept entries, longTail, reasons, metrics, stats) + serializable + cross-run determinism; realistic extractor mix (Slow Cinema + Obscure Auteur survive; Drama cut by generic_weight; Blockbuster cut by mainstream_no_excess). Four test corrections pinned against real semantics: float rounding of excess moved into the module, R3 boundary case re-sited below the mainstream ceiling, the fallback valve explicitly disabled in rule-isolation tests, and one corrupted assertion expression cleaned. npm test 482/482 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 470), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

## Task 57 — entity uniqueness coefficient from global Qloo popularity

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-16

Deliverable: server/src/engine/UniquenessCoefficient.js — the mathematical uniqueness score of an entity derived from its GLOBAL popularity in the Qloo sample. INFORMATION-THEORETIC MODEL (pure, zero imports): popularity percentile p is treated as the probability of meeting the entity at random in the sample; raw surprisal bits(p) = -log2(p); the coefficient U(p) = log2(1/p) / log2(1/minProbability) with minProbability 1e-6 (exported default) — bounded [0,1], strictly monotone decreasing in p (more famous, less unique, always), exact landmark values at decimal percentiles (U(1e-k) = k/6; U(1e-3) = 0.5 exactly; U(1e-6) = 1; U(1) = 0); linear 1-p scales rejected in the doc because Qloo percentiles cluster low and rarity differences live where only the log resolves them. Four-band tier ladder (exported UNIQUENESS_TIERS, inclusive minima): microtail >= 0.75, long_tail >= 0.35, midstream >= 0.15, else mainstream. HONEST DATA HANDLING mirrors task 56: gateway-dropped p === 0 -> coefficient 1, reason zero_popularity, tier microtail ("rarer than measurable"); missing popularity -> null with no_popularity (an unmeasured entity is never silently scored); out-of-range p (< 0 or > 1) -> null with invalid_popularity (malformed upstream reported, not guessed). Outputs rounded to 6 decimals (float-stable JSON/contracts). annotateUniqueness(candidates) batch-annotates extractor outputs (tasks 51-55) with {coefficient, bits, tier, reason} per entry (frozen copies, order-preserving, originals untouched) plus stats {count, measured, unmeasured, tiers, meanCoefficient} (mean over measured, null when none). Options validated (TypeError on minProbability outside (0,1] — programmer error).

Verification: server/test/uniquenessCoefficient.test.js 11/11 — landmark math (U(1)=0 mainstream; 1/6 and 1/3 approximated against round6; U(1e-3)=0.5 exact; U(1e-6)=1 microtail); 10-point monotonicity chain; raw bits (0.25 -> 2, 0.5 -> 1, 1 -> 0, floor -> log2(1e6) within round6); input forms (raw number, object, missing field, null, non-string); honest handling (no_popularity for missing/null/garbage, zero_popularity max-coefficient, invalid_popularity for -0.1 and 1.5); tier boundary inclusivity via back-constructed p = 2^(-c*maxBits) at 0.75/0.35/0.15 and just below each; validation TypeErrors x4 on both functions; batch annotation (4-entry mix: mainstream hit, U=0.5 long_tail classic, microtail 1e-5 gem, unmeasured entry; order preserved; exact stats incl. tier counts and derived mean); frozen outputs + JSON float-stability + cross-run determinism; empty/degenerate inputs; composition with the task-56 LongTailFilter (survivors annotated, the deeper cut scores strictly higher uniqueness). Three test expectations corrected against real semantics (round6 makes exact 1/6/1/3 asserts approximations; bits tolerance widened to 1e-6; U(0.002) is long_tail ~0.45, not microtail). npm test 493/493 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 482), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

## Task 61 — non-obvious-first sorting of the output array

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-16

Deliverable: server/src/engine/NonObviousSorter.js — re-ranks the output array with PRIORITY FOR NON-OBVIOUS BUT MATHEMATICALLY CONFIRMED high-level correlations. CONFIRMED SURPRISE index composed from the existing math stack (zero new formulas, sibling imports): confirmation = connectionScoreOf (task-60 priority chain), excess = max(0, excessAffinity) (task-56: relevance beyond fame — only the positive part is non-obvious evidence), obscurity = U(popularity) (task-57); surprise = confirmation * (1 + excessWeight*excess + rarityWeight*obscurity) — a RELATIVE sort key bounded by 1 + excessWeight + rarityWeight, not a probability. Discrimination properties pinned by tests: at equal confirmation the higher-excess entry wins; a weaker-but-specific correlation (0.6 confirmation, near-microtail popularity) outranks a fame-explained mass hit (0.9 confirmation); entries with NO measurable confirmation (popularity alone is not a connection) get surprise null and sort LAST in input order — unmeasured never outranks measured. Ordering: surprise desc -> confirmation desc -> INPUT ORDER (deterministic stable sort, no RNG); the sort RE-RANKS ONLY — no entry is dropped (filtering is tasks 56/58). Output: frozen {...entry, sorting: {surprise, confirmation, excess, obscurity}} + stats {count, measured, unmeasured}. Options excessWeight/rarityWeight in [0, 10] validated (TypeError); zero weights collapse surprise to pure confirmation (documented). DOCUMENTED LIMITS (review-gate honesty): single capitalized tokens mid-sentence and unquoted article-led names are deliberately NOT extracted — quote to be checked. Verdict frozen {ok, checked, matched, knownCount, hallucinated:[{mention, form, index}]}; ok=false => review required. Options includeTitleCase/minRunLength/maxMentionLength validated by passthrough defaults. Pure engine module: no I/O, no clocks, no RNG.

## Task 66 — single-aesthetic-tonality coherence pipeline for connections

Status: DONE

Deliverable: server/src/engine/TonalityEngine.js — the coherence-check pipeline for the obtained connections under the principle of a SINGLE AESTHETIC TONALITY. FOUR DETERMINISTIC STAGES (each exported): (1) classifyTone — maps a connection's aesthetic evidence (task-65 shared markers + the target's tag names/ids, ONE haystack per tag to avoid name+id double counting) onto FIVE exported opposing POLES via a tuning lexicon: dark<->light, melancholic<->uplifting, minimal<->ornate, futuristic<->traditional, intense<->calm; per axis value = (positive-negative)/(positive+negative) in [-1,1]; axes without evidence are null; a connection without ANY tone evidence is honestly `unclassified`, never forced onto a tone; (2) coherenceCentroid — evidence-weighted mean per axis (more evidence pulls harder); (3) deviation — mean |v-centroid| over comparable axes, EXCLUDING contradiction axes (a bimodal axis has no meaningful centroid); (4) verdict — outlier when deviation >= 0.6 (exported); contradiction when an axis holds >= 2 strong voices on BOTH flanks (contradictionPole 0.5, contradictionMinFlank 2) — KEY SEMANTICS: a LONE dissenter is an outlier, TWO CAMPS are a contradiction that exempts its axis from deviation. coherent = classified >= 1 && no outliers && no contradictions; everything unclassifiable lands in stats.unclassified (the shared honesty rule of tasks 56-65). Options validated (TypeError). Pure engine module: no I/O, no clocks, no RNG.

Verification: server/test/tonalityEngine.test.js 9/9 — five-pole classification (dark -1 with evidence words, mixed 0, no-evidence axis null, unclassified never forced); evidence-weighted centroid exact math (-2+1)/3 = -0.333333; coherent dark set (zero deviations, no contradictions, exact stats); lone dissenter (3 dark vs 1 cheerful: centroid -0.636, dissent 1.636 >= 0.6 outlier, darks 0.364 below, ONE light-flank voice -> not a contradiction, stats.outliers 1); two camps (3v3: contradiction axis dark_light with BOTH flanks named, axis exempt -> deviations null, outliers 0); honest unclassified reporting (classified 2 / unclassified 1, coherent still true); validation TypeErrors + empty-input semantics (nothing classified is NOT coherent); frozen/serializable/cross-run deterministic outputs; composition with task-65 annotateConnections output (sharedMarkers + tags carry the tone, dark-leaning centroid, coherent verdict). ## Task 67 — adaptive affinity threshold for rare and ultra-niche entities

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-16

Deliverable: server/src/engine/AdaptiveThreshold.js — edge-case handling: AUTOMATIC AFFINITY THRESHOLD RELIEF for rare/ultra-niche entities. THE PROBLEM: Qloo affinity is a sparse-graph measurement — ultra-rare entities have fewer co-occurrences, so even perfect taste matches arrive with systematically weaker raw affinity; a fixed gate (task 52's 0.65 contract) rejects exactly the long tail the project exists to surface. THE SCHEDULE: T(entity) = max(minThreshold, T0 * (1 - relief * U(popularity))) with the task-57 uniqueness coefficient; defaults base 0.65 / relief 0.5 / minThreshold 0.2 (full relief at the support floor -> 0.325); monotone in rarity, never above base, floor-bounded (below the floor the graph signal is noise and rarity cannot excuse it). HONEST DATA HANDLING (shared rule of tasks 56-66): missing popularity = UNPROVEN rarity -> NO relief (threshold = base, rarity null); missing affinity never passes (reason no_affinity). Compat pin: relief 0 reproduces the legacy FIXED gate exactly. Surfaces: affinityThreshold (the schedule with relief/tier/floorApplied diagnostics), passesThreshold (verdict with affinity/threshold/margin/reason), filterByAdaptiveThreshold (order-preserving batch, auditable rejection ledger {id, name, reason, affinity, threshold, margin, rarity}, stats {input, kept, rejected, noAffinity, relieved, meanRelief}). Options validated (TypeError). Pure engine module reusing tasks 56/57 metrics; no I/O, no clocks, no RNG.

Verification: server/test/adaptiveThreshold.test.js 8/8 — schedule landmarks (T(1)=0.65 relief 0; T(1e-3)=0.4875 at U=0.5; T(1e-6)=0.325 microtail tier; 6-point strict monotonicity chain; never above base, object input); no-proof-no-relief (missing popularity -> base gate, rarity null); floor behavior (relief 1 + minThreshold 0.2 -> threshold 0.2, floorApplied true; defaults below floor never); legacy compat pin (relief 0 -> fixed base); verdicts (the SAME affinity 0.4 flips pass/fail purely on rarity — the task in one pin; margins exact; no_affinity reason; task-52 correlation alias); batch ledger + exact stats (relieved counts rarity-measured entries, meanRelief = mean U over measured); validation TypeErrors + empty input + cross-run determinism + frozen serializable records; composition with task-56 LongTailFilter (both positive-excess survivors pass the Via Negativa cut, only the niche one clears the RELIEVED gate while the famous one misses the near-base threshold). ## Task 75 — async chain optimization: Promise.allSettled orchestration

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-16

Deliverables:
- server/src/services/parallelTasks.js — the async orchestration utilities keeping the main thread responsive. runParallelTasks(tasks, {concurrency}): heterogeneous {name, run} tasks run CONCURRENTLY through a bounded worker pool with the Promise.allSettled contract per slot (a rejection is captured into its record — never aborts siblings, never rejects the batch) joined via an explicit Promise.allSettled over inert lanes; results index-aligned (completion order never leaks — repo-wide determinism rule); concurrency defaults to full parallelism, a finite value caps in-flight work against loop flooding. chunkedProcess(items, handler, {chunkSize}): the CPU-blocking counterpart — batch slices with an explicit setImmediate YIELD between chunks so long computations cannot starve I/O callbacks; order preserved; handler errors reject (programming errors, not data).
- server/src/services/dossierLayers.js — extractAllLayers(anchor, clients, options): the five extraction layers (tasks 51-55) as ONE CONCURRENT WAVE through runParallelTasks. The audit finding: the golden scenarios awaited the layers sequentially (five serial network round-trips where each service only parallelized its own legs); the new composition collapses wall time from sum(RTT) to ~max(RTT) on real upstreams. Failure model mirrors the services: a rejecting layer lands in failures {layer, message} with coverage[layer]=false and layers[layer]=null (never partial); shared injected clients serve all five services.
- server/test/agentCycleBudget.test.js (task 74) now measures the OPTIMIZED path — the budget cycle uses extractAllLayers.

## Task 76 — memory isolation: GC after each dossier generation cycle

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-16

Deliverables:
- server/src/services/memoryHygiene.js — the GC hygiene contract for generation cycles. REALITY-CHECKED design: portable Node cannot force collection (global.gc exists only under --expose-gc), so the module is best-effort AND honest — collectGarbage() calls the hook when exposed (optionally a second lazy sweep after an event-loop turn, deep default) and otherwise returns performed:false/reason 'gc_not_exposed', never pretending; cycleMemorySnapshot() reports heapUsed/heapTotal/rss bytes; isolateCycle(run, options) is THE wrapper: executes one cycle, guarantees ONLY the returned record escapes (cycle intermediates become unreachable), runs GC when the host allows and reports {before, after, gcPerformed, gcSweeps, heapDeltaBytes, rssDeltaBytes} + a pressure flag when post-isolation heap growth exceeds maxHeapGrowthBytes (default 8 MB; the module reports, the executor decides). Program errors -> TypeError.
- server/test-support/fullCycleRunner.js — the full agent generation cycle extracted from the budget gate so the task-74 perf gate and the task-76 memory gate measure THE SAME code path (moved out of server/test because the node runner executes every .js under test dirs).
- server/test/agentCycleBudget.test.js — refactored onto the shared runner (measured path also uses task-75's concurrent layer wave).
- package.json: new script "test:gc" = node --expose-gc --test server/test/memoryIsolation.test.js — the REAL-GC path is CI-verifyable.

Verification: server/test/memoryIsolation.test.js 6/6 in BOTH modes (npm test without the hook, npm run test:gc with it) — honest collectGarbage reporting in both host modes (performed false/true; the --expose-gc property is writable-but-not-configurable, so tests shadow it by assignment with deterministic restore); deep double-sweep counting; snapshot byte-level shape; isolateCycle (only the result escapes, GC invoked after the cycle, deltas finite, frozen records, pressure flag tripped by a 1-byte threshold and calm under a generous one); validation TypeErrors; and the FULL generation cycle x5 through isolation: every cycle completed, deltas reported, ZERO pressure flags in both modes — with the hook: heap deltas 50-181KB per cycle (no cross-cycle leakage); without: V8's own ~1.7MB sawtooth, honestly reported. Two development fixes: helper import paths corrected after the move, and the gc-shadowing helper switched from defineProperty (throws on non-configurable props) to plain assignment. npm test 666/666 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 660), npm run test:gc 6/6, npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

## Task 95 — taste-vector inversion algorithm (minimal / negative correlation)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-17

Deliverable: server/src/engine/TasteInverter.js — THE TASTE-VECTOR INVERSION ALGORITHM as a mode-agnostic engine module: selects entities with the MINIMAL or NEGATIVE correlation coefficient in the Qloo graph. THE COEFFICIENT CHAIN (exported coefficientOf, priority order): 1) a DECLARED SIGNED `correlation` in [-1, 1] — present-but-invalid values (non-finite, out of range) are DATA CORRUPTION: the row is rejected as UNMEASURED (reason coefficient_out_of_range) and never silently salvaged; 2) the UNSIGNED task-60 chain (connectionScoreOf: affinity -> correlation -> index -> weight), range-checked to [0, 1] with the same corruption rule; 3) nothing measurable -> no_measurable_coefficient; a row is never coerced. TWO DIVERGENCE CONVENTIONS, both bounded [0, 1]: SIGNED (1 - c) / 2 — a proven negative (c < 0) is the strongest antithesis evidence (c = 0 -> 50%, c = -1 -> 100%); UNSIGNED 1 - a — the Qloo affinity channel has NO negative territory: a = 0 is a proven ZERO correlation (negativeCorrelation honestly null — absence of evidence is never claimed as opposition); mixed-input pools report coefficientDomain (signed | unsigned | mixed | empty). SELECTION (the task's minimal OR negative): `lowest` (default) = the `take` strongest divergences; `negative` = ONLY proven signed negatives — an unsigned-only pool yields an HONEST empty selection. ORDERING: divergence DESC, popularity DESC (a mainstream blindspot pick is the strongest conflict/risk material), name, id. REFACORING: AntithesisMode.buildAntithesisBlock (task 94) now DELEGATES the inversion math to this module (the mode keeps only the report framing: uniqueness annotation, the matrix/lanterns, sponsorship risks) — one proven algorithm, zero duplicated math; ANTITHESIS_TIERS gained `negative_correlation` (proven signed c < 0 > zero_affinity > lowest_correlation); antithesis records now report BOTH conventions honestly (unsigned rows carry `affinity`, signed rows carry `correlation` — never coerced into each other); the block echoes coefficientDomain and stats.negatives. Backward compatible: the task-94 pinned behavior on unsigned-only vectors is bit-identical.

## Task 96 — antipode filtration (cut accidental noise, concentrate on conscious dissonance)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-17

Deliverable: server/src/engine/DissonanceFilter.js — FILTERING OF ANTIPODES over the inverted vector (task 95): cuts ACCIDENTAL NOISE and concentrates the report on entities that cause CONSCIOUS cultural dissonance. THE PREMISE: a weak correlation alone does not prove dissonance — an entity the audience never encountered (missing popularity) or a rare-tail pick with a low coefficient is GRAPH NOISE, not opposition (you cannot consciously reject what you have never met). Two independent axes decide. EVIDENCE (from the inverter's own fields): proven (a signed negative — the Qloo API itself asserts opposition; kept REGARDLESS of encounter, the graph's assertion stands alone) > zero (a proven zero affinity) > weak (the weakest positive correlation). ENCOUNTER (the task-57 uniqueness tiers over popularity — ONE tier table, no parallel vocabulary): a row counts as `broad` when its rarity tier is at or above `minEncounterTier` (default `mainstream` = Qloo popularity >= ~0.13, the broadly-recognized band — the task-57 normalized coefficient lands mainstream there; lowering the bar to `midstream` widens the net; unmeasured popularity is never encounter evidence). DECISION MATRIX: proven + any -> KEEP proven_dissonance; zero + broad -> KEEP measured_dissonance; zero + obscure/unmeasured -> CUT obscure_unproven / unmeasured_encounter; weak + broad -> KEEP minimal_correlation when keepMinimal (default true) else CUT weak_correlation; weak + obscure/unmeasured -> CUT obscure_unproven / unmeasured_encounter. Every cut lands in the LEDGER with a machine-readable reason — nothing dropped silently; KEPT records preserve the input (divergence) order — the filter classifies, never re-ranks. REFACORING: buildAntithesisBlock now runs the filter between the inversion and the matrix — the blindspot matrix, conflict drivers and sponsorship risks are built from CONSCIOUS-dissonance records ONLY; the block gains a `dissonance` section (minEncounterTier, keepMinimal, per-class counts, per-reason counts, the cut ledger) and stats.noiseCut; runAntithesis exposes options.keepMinimal (boolean) and options.minEncounterTier (one of the four tiers), both validated with runAntithesis-prefixed TypeErrors; the render gains a NOISE CUT line (names + humanized reasons). DELIBERATE DEFAULT CHANGE: the mode's default mainstream bar now noise-cuts midstream-popularity rows the task-94 report used to place — the evolution task 96 mandates (concentration on conscious dissonance); the task-94 tests were updated to pin the new default (Tie A/Tie B at popularity 0.1 are ledgered as noise, not silently dropped) and keepMinimal/minEncounterTier restore the wider net on request.

Verification: server/test/dissonanceFilter.test.js 7/7 — the encounter axis (mainstream 0.9 -> broad; midstream 0.05/0.01 -> obscure under the default bar; absent/null -> unmeasured; the midstream bar loosening) and the tier pin [microtail, long_tail, midstream, mainstream]; the FULL decision matrix, all nine evidence x encounter cells (proven kept even on obscure/unmeasured popularity — the API assertion stands); the options (keepMinimal=false cuts the weak class with reason weak_correlation; the default mainstream bar cuts a midstream zero while the loosened midstream bar keeps it as measured_dissonance; the TypeError matrix on both classify and filter surfaces); the filter itself (divergence-order preservation, the three-class + three-reason ledger, the 6-field stats pin, deep-freeze on kept AND cut); determinism; the MODE INTEGRATION (the matrix runs on conscious records only, the noise rows ledgered as dissonance.cut, stats.noiseCut, the strict keepMinimal=false path, the NOISE CUT render line with humanized reasons, zero emojis, the buildAntithesisBlock TypeError bounds); the REAL runAntithesis (dissonance rides the block, the session stays terminal at DELIVERED/COMPLETED, selected <= measurable and noiseCut <= measurable as the perDomain-capped invariants, the strict options pass through, the TypeError bounds). ## Task 99 — calculateCulturalDistance (two-entity comparison)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-17

Deliverable: server/src/engine/CulturalDistance.js — `calculateCulturalDistance(entityA, entityB)`: a deterministic mechanism for comparing TWO arbitrary cultural entities on a 0..1 distance scale (0 = indistinguishable taste, 1 = maximal MEASURED distance). THE HONEST PREMISE: the system never invents a direct similarity the graph did not measure — the distance is a RENORMALIZED weighted mean (task-48 discipline) over the evidence axes the caller actually has. TWO AXES: tags (weight 0.40) = 1 minus the task-59 rarity-weighted tag kinship between the two entities' tag sets (a metadata axis: shared cultural vocabulary); vector (weight 0.60) = the mean absolute deviation between the two entities' per-domain AFFINITY PROFILES over the SHARED domains only (a direct taste axis: who the graph says each resonates with) — domains present on only one side are honestly not comparable and contribute NOTHING. An axis with no evidence on one side is MISSING (reason no_tags / no_shared_domains) and RENORMALIZED OUT, never scored as zero; when NOTHING is comparable the distance is honestly null ("stays uncomputed"). The readout is banded via the exported DISTANCE_BANDS (a documented tuning contract, not psychology): kindred [0, 0.2), adjacent [0.2, 0.5), divergent [0.5, 0.8), antipodal [0.8, 1]. SYMMETRIC by construction (tag kinship and absolute deviation are symmetric; component order fixed). Adapter profileFromVector(vector) converts the task-50 fetchAffinityVector result into a profile {domain: meanAffinity} — error legs and pages without a single finite affinity are EXCLUDED, never zero-filled. Validated weights override (unknown key / negative / NaN / all-zero -> TypeError). Pure module, no I/O, no clocks, no RNG.

Verification: server/test/culturalDistance.test.js 7/7 — BOTH AXES with pinned math (tags: shared {gothic} of union 3 = kinship 0.333333 -> distance 0.666667 with the "1 shared of 3 union tag(s)" evidence string; vector: |0.9-0.3| and |0.7-0.9| over 2 shared domains -> 0.4; the combined distance = the renormalized mean 0.5066668 -> 51%, band divergent, the between-names pin); the RENORMALIZED WEIGHTING (no tags -> the vector axis alone IS the distance 0.4 with the no_tags ledger; profiles sharing no domain -> the vector axis unmeasurable with no_shared_domains while identical tags yield an honest 0); the HONEST NULL (no tags and no profiles -> distance null, band null, both axes missing, "stays uncomputed"); the BAND readout at the exact boundaries (0 -> kindred; 0.2 -> adjacent, the boundary belongs to the upper band; 0.5 -> divergent; 0.8 and 1 -> antipodal; null -> null); SYMMETRY (components and distance equal under swap), the weights overrides isolating each axis (vector-only -> 0.4, tags-only -> 0.666667), and the TypeError matrix (negative/NaN/unknown/all-zero weights, null/array entities, null vector); the profileFromVector adapter (music averaged to 0.6, books to 1 over the single finite affinity, the EMPTY film page and the ERROR dining leg EXCLUDED — never zero-filled; deep-freeze); determinism (deep-equal results) and zero-emoji purity on both the measured and null summaries. npm test 818/818 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 811), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

## Task 102 — semantic Markdown export of the dossier

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-17

Deliverable: server/src/formatters/MarkdownExporter.js — SEMANTIC MARKDOWN export of the generated dossier (the task-101 unified document). CONTENT VS MARKUP DISCIPLINE: the plain-text section bodies come from the EXISTING render* functions (one source of truth for CONTENT — no prose is re-implemented); this exporter owns the MARKUP layer and upgrades the ASCII layout to clean, semantic Markdown: the dossier title -> H1 (`#`), the anchor/request line -> a blockquote (`>`), the 60-char ASCII rule -> a Markdown horizontal rule (`---`), each section title -> H2 (`##`), an ALL-CAPS lead label -> bold (`**LABEL:** rest`), and `   - ` / `      - ` bullets -> real Markdown list items with ONE preserved nesting level (top-level at column 0, nested at 2 spaces — NEVER 4, which CommonMark reads as a code block; development fix). SAFETY DISCIPLINE (no prose mangling): only ALL-CAPS lead labels are bolded (free narrative text never matches that shape, so the `narrative` section passes through VERBATIM) and only bullet lines are re-indented; everything else is emitted as-is — the exporter marks up, it never rewrites. An optional YAML FRONT MATTER (`options.frontMatter`) emits `mode`/`output` for static-site and MCP consumers. The input is EITHER a formatDossier() result (has `title` + `sections`) or a mode bundle (has `mode`) — the latter is formatted first; `markdownFromBundle(bundle, options)` is the explicit one-call form. ## Task 103 — standardized JSON export (third-party API pipelines)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-17

Deliverable: server/src/formatters/JsonExporter.js — STANDARDIZED JSON export of a mode's results for third-party pipelines over the API. THE CONTRACT, NOT THE ACCIDENT: unlike a bare JSON.stringify(bundle), this emits a CLOSED, VERSIONED envelope (fixed key order, an explicit data/meta split, a json-safe transform) so a consumer's parser does not break when the internal bundle grows a new field. Only the keys on the exported lists ever leave: fixed (schema `suto-osnova.dossier/v1`, version, mode, output, requestId, seed, entity — the stable identity triple id/name/category); data (the DOMAIN PAYLOAD, exported DATA_KEYS: scene, ranked, layers, connections, tonality, graph, weights, audience, curation, resonance, foodZones, afterparty, antithesis, selection); meta (the PROVENANCE/LEDGER, exported META_KEYS: resolution, expansion, chain, coverage, synthesis, validation, failures, stats). Anything else on the bundle (the internal AgentSession FSM record, scratch fields) is DROPPED BY DESIGN — the raw bundle stays the internal surface, this is the external contract. JSON-SAFETY: a recursive transform maps non-finite numbers to null (JSON cannot hold Infinity/NaN — an honest null, never a silently-corrupted 0), drops undefined/function/symbol values from objects, and keeps array positions (a dropped slot becomes null). DETERMINISM (no clocks): there is NO timestamp unless the CALLER injects `options.now` — a pure run stays byte-identical; an API caller wanting `generatedAt` supplies it explicitly. Surfaces: exportJson(bundle, options) -> the frozen envelope; stringifyJson(bundle, options) -> the JSON string (options.indent default 2, 0 = compact single-line). Pinned by server/test/jsonExporter.test.js.

Verification: server/test/jsonExporter.test.js 6/6 — the CLOSED, VERSIONED envelope over a REAL activation bundle (schema/version/mode output-mapped/requestId/seed; the entity reduced to the stable {id,name,category} triple with no internal extras; data carrying the audience+curation payload and meta carrying stats+failures+expansion+chain; `session` proven ABSENT from the envelope); the CLOSED-CONTRACT GUARANTEE (a tainted bundle with an internalSecret key leaks nothing — every envelope key is on the allowed list and every data/meta key is on the exported DATA_KEYS/META_KEYS); JSON-SAFETY (Infinity -> an honest null, undefined dropped from objects, array positions preserved) confirmed in both the object and string round-trips; DETERMINISM (byte-identical strings; the object and string forms agree; the compact indent:0 form is a single line that parses to the same object; zero emojis); the caller-injected timestamp and its honest ABSENCE (generatedAt present only when options.now is given); the antithesis payload (`data.antithesis`, output cultural_blindspot_report) and the TypeError matrix (null/non-object/non-string-mode bundles, a non-string now, out-of-range indent). npm test 843/843 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 837), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

## Task 114 — GET /api/categories (supported categories metadata)

Status: DONE

GET /api/categories returns the SUPPORTED CATEGORIES (the five mandatory agent domains) with their full registry metadata, so an API consumer can discover what cultural domains the agent understands and how to address them (aliases) before calling a generate route. The response is a pure, static projection of the domainMapper's DOMAIN_REGISTRY — no Qloo network, no key, no per-request data — so it carries a cache header (public, max-age=300). Each category exposes id (the canonical domain id used in requests), label (the human-readable name), aliases (friendly names resolveDomain accepts), searchTypes (the Qloo entity types for GET /search types=), insightTypes (the types for GET /v2/insights filter.type=), and tagFamilies (the tag URN families that qualify an entity of a matching type as part of the domain — dining and fashion require these; music/books/film map directly). The route is registered next to health (a GET with no body, so it skips JSON parsing), and the app.js docblock route list documents it.

## Task 115 — lightweight JSON Schema request validation

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-17

Deliverables: server/src/schemaValidator.js (a dependency-free JSON-Schema-subset validator) + the GENERATE_REQUEST_SCHEMA in generate.js + the validateBody refactor. WHY A SUBSET, NOT A LIBRARY: the project's discipline is zero framework bloat and a measured dependency budget; the generate routes need only a handful of keywords, so the validator is literal JSON Schema (a standard format), adds ZERO dependencies, and stays decoupled from Zod (an MCP-layer concern). SUPPORTED KEYWORDS: type (object/string/number/integer/array/boolean/null), required, properties (recursed), additionalProperties:false, enum, minLength/maxLength, minimum/maximum, minItems/maxItems, pattern, plus a PROJECT `errorCode` extension that names the machine-readable code surfaced when a property fails (so a route maps a schema failure to its HTTP envelope code without string-matching messages). validateSchema(data, schema) -> {valid, errors:[{path,code,message}]}, pure and total; a type mismatch stops descent (cannot descend into a wrong type); errors are reported in declaration order (required first, then properties) so throwing the FIRST error yields a deterministic, stable code. The generate.js validateBody now runs the schema (GENERATE_REQUEST_SCHEMA: seed string, format enum json|markdown|html, options object; the free-form options are validated by each MODE's own validateOptions) and preserves the exact HTTP codes (INVALID_SEED / INVALID_FORMAT / INVALID_OPTIONS / INVALID_BODY via the root errorCode) + a post-schema semantic trim check for the whitespace-only seed.

Verification: server/test/schemaValidator.test.js 6/6 — TYPE MATCHING across all seven types incl. the number/integer finite checks (NaN and Infinity rejected) and the array-not-object rule; REQUIRED / properties RECURSION / paths / additionalProperties:false (unknown key rejected with its path); ENUM / length / range / items / pattern; the ERRORCODE EXTENSION + DETERMINISTIC ERROR ORDER (required-first, declaration order, the root code for a non-object); the GENERATE_REQUEST_SCHEMA mapping to the EXACT route codes ({}->INVALID_SEED, bad format->INVALID_FORMAT, array options->INVALID_OPTIONS, numeric seed->INVALID_SEED, null/array body->INVALID_BODY, a free-form options OBJECT + a valid format pass); determinism + the empty-schema edge (accepts anything). REGRESSION: all 14 generate-route + 3 categories-route tests pass unchanged under the validateBody refactor (the codes are byte-identical). npm test 911/911 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 905), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

## Task 116 — inbound per-client spam protection for the generate routes

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-17

Deliverables: server/src/middleware/rateLimit.js (ClientRateLimiter + writeRateLimited) + the app.js wiring + two env vars (INBOUND_RATE_LIMIT default 30, INBOUND_RATE_WINDOW_MS default 60000) in envSchema. DISTINCT from the OUTBOUND RateLimiter (services/rateLimiter.js, which protects the Qloo API-key quota): this guards the SERVER against one client spamming repeated generate requests — a generate run is expensive (a full cross-domain pipeline + upstream calls), so a FIXED WINDOW per client key fails fast on a burst. DESIGN: clientKey(req) identifies the client by the FIRST X-Forwarded-For hop (a proxy prepends; the leftmost is the real client) else the socket remote address; each key holds {count, resetAt} and resets past its window; maxRequests per windowMs, and a burst beyond it is REJECTED (never queued — failing fast is cheaper than running the pipeline); the map is BOUNDED (maxClients 10000) — expired entries are evicted first, then the earliest-reset entry, so a distributed spoof-IP flood cannot grow memory without limit. check(key, now) accepts an injected clock for deterministic tests. The 429 is written by writeRateLimited (a transport concern the limiter owns directly): the RATE_LIMITED envelope + a Retry-After header + X-Request-Id; it is crash-safe (a live stream is destroyed, not corrupted). WIRING: one limiter per createHttpHandler instance (a fresh bucket set per server); the limiter runs BEFORE body parsing for the /api/generate/ prefix, so EVERY generate attempt counts — malformed or not — then the request is parsed and dispatched to the right mode handler; health and categories are NOT limited (no expensive pipeline behind them).

Verification: server/test/rateLimitRoute.test.js 7/7 — the LIMITER CLASS with an injected clock (counts within a window, blocks past the limit with a Retry-After, resets past the window); the CLIENT KEY (the leftmost X-Forwarded-For hop, the socket fallback, 'unknown'); the BOUNDED MAP (eviction keeps it <= maxClients; the stats snapshot; the TypeError bounds); the HTTP ENFORCEMENT (the burst beyond the limit -> 429 RATE_LIMITED with the Retry-After + X-Request-Id headers, the first 3 consuming the window); PER-CLIENT BUCKETS (a different X-Forwarded-For gets a fresh window while the same client stays exhausted); ALL THREE generate routes protected while health + categories stay UNLIMITED (10 requests each -> all 200); the 429 writer's CRASH SAFETY on a live stream. REGRESSION: all 14 generate-route + 3 categories-route + 8 health-route tests pass unchanged (the default 30/60s limit does not trip them); the two new env vars pass the env-schema verify tests. npm test 918/918 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 911), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

## Task 117 — dossier generation time budget (most complex mode + LLM)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-17

Deliverable: server/test/generationBudget.test.js — a budget gate verifying the DOSSIER GENERATION TIME in the MOST COMPLEX mode (Mode 1: the worldbuilder — the five-domain expansion, the five extraction layers, AND the LLM narrative synthesis, the worst-case generation path) against the PRD acceptance criterion (< 4.5s for the full 5-domain cycle). OFFLINE HONESTY: the Qloo + LLM clients are STUBBED (zero network), so the measured time is the pipeline's own COMPUTE plus the simulated LLM latency. The gate proves two things: (1) the COMPUTE-ONLY full run (a 0-latency LLM stub, 5 measured iterations after a warm-up that absorbs JIT/import costs) is far under the budget AND leaves headroom for real network + LLM time; (2) a run with a REALISTIC simulated LLM latency (~1.5s) still completes under 4.5s AND the latency is actually felt (the call was not optimized away) — generation INCLUDING an LLM call fits the budget. MEASURED (this machine): compute-only Mode 1 + LLM = 32.2 / 16.2 / 15.1 / 16.8 / 17.7 ms (worst 32.2ms, mean 19.6ms); with a 1500ms simulated LLM call = 1515.2ms. The pipeline compute overhead is negligible (~20ms); the PRD 4.5s budget is dominated by network + LLM latency and is comfortably met (a 1.5s LLM call leaves ~3s of headroom). The LLM path is asserted to genuinely run on every iteration (synthesis.attempted true, narrative text present).

Verification: server/test/generationBudget.test.js 3/3 — the WARM-UP run (excluded from measurement); the COMPUTE-ONLY gate (5 iterations, each asserting the LLM synthesis ran + produced narrative + ranked picks; the worst-of-5 fits < 4.5s AND < 4.5s - 1.5s, proving headroom for a real LLM call); the INCLUDING-LLM gate (a ~1.5s simulated LLM latency, the total < 4.5s, and elapsed >= the simulated latency so the call was not elided). Stable across repeated runs (the worst-of-5 guard absorbs scheduling jitter). npm test 921/921 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 918), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

## Task 118 — full mode isolation in the codebase

Status: DONE

Deliverable: server/test/modeIsolation.test.js — pins that the THREE agent modes (worldbuilder / experiential / antithesis) are COMPLETELY ISOLATED from one another. FIVE ISOLATION PROPERTIES, each verified against the real mode runners over the shared offline stubs: (1) SEQUENTIAL INDEPENDENCE — running A, then B, then C leaves A's result byte-identical to running A fresh (no cross-run contamination of any module state); (2) CONCURRENT NON-INTERFERENCE — running all three at once via Promise.all yields results deep-equal to the sequential runs (the requestId and every bundle field are deterministic — `wb-/ac-/ta-<entity.id>`, no clock, no RNG — so execution order cannot matter and there is no shared mutable state to race on); (3) BUNDLE IMMUTABILITY — each mode's bundle is deep-frozen and carries its OWN identity with no cross-mode fields (worldbuilder has no `antithesis` block; antithesis has no `curation` and no `layers`); (4) DISPATCHER INDEPENDENCE — the MODES registry is frozen (a mutation attempt throws TypeError in strict ESM) and each mode's run plan is distinct and self-contained (worldbuilder/antithesis share the five layers, activation the three; modes 1-2 select non-obvious affinity, mode 3 inverts to lowest_correlation with the honest null thresholds; the plans do not alias each other's arrays); (5) OPTION ISOLATION — an option valid for one mode does not bleed into another (a worldbuilder run is immune to any experiential option), and an invalid option for one mode rejects ONLY that mode while the others stay fully functional. This complements the task-76 memoryIsolation gate (which pins GC hygiene, not mode-to-mode isolation).

Verification: server/test/modeIsolation.test.js 5/5 — the SEQUENTIAL INDEPENDENCE deep-equal (A/B/C/A'); the CONCURRENT Promise.all vs sequential deep-equal for all three modes; BUNDLE IMMUTABILITY + the per-mode identity/no-cross-field assertions; the frozen DISPATCHER registry (mutation throws) + the distinct self-contained plans (layers, selection, thresholds, non-aliasing domains); OPTION ISOLATION (a worldbuilder run unchanged by experiential options; a rejected activation run leaves worldbuilder functional). Stable across repeated runs (deterministic bundles). npm test 926/926 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 921), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

The task's proposed stack contained JetBrains Mono; per the project owner's directive it was REMOVED (JetBrains: founded 2000 in Saint Petersburg, Russian-origin R&D). The retained members are US-provenance: IBM Plex — IBM (USA, designed by Bold Monday); Courier New — Monotype (USA, originally drawn for IBM); the generic `monospace` guarantees a fixed-width face everywhere. Consumption: (1) the TECHNICAL VIEW — a new rule `.output-text[data-format='json']` styles the JSON envelope (scores, resonance %, ids, numeric fields) with the mono token; ui.js renderJson now tags the <pre> with data-format="json" and renderText REMOVES the attribute, so the markdown/analytical view stays serif (typography.css cascade: [data-format] specificity beats the base .output-text serif rule); (2) the instrument chrome — main.css body previously held a HARDCODED mono stack (ui-monospace, Cascadia, SF Mono, Menlo, Consolas — contradicting its own header contract); it now styles via var(--font-mono). The typography.css header documents the stack's provenance and the ban (obliquely — the banned vendor name appears NOWHERE under client/, enforced by the gate). No external font transport (unchanged contract).

Verification: tests/typography.test.js extended to 7 tests (was 5) — the new gates: (a) STACKS VERBATIM (both --font-serif and --font-mono must equal their exact ordered triples); (b) MONO CONSUMPTION — the JSON technical view is a dedicated rule styling with var(--font-mono), body styles via the TOKEN, and main.css holds NO hardcoded font stack of its own (comments stripped, var() refs removed, any literal stack fails); (c) THE BAN — the string 'jetbrains' must not occur anywhere under client/ (even in a comment; reworded the ban note in typography.css to satisfy it). Plus the retained gates: serif scope (h1-h6 + .output-text only), no external font transport (network @import / remote url() / remote @font-face), the main.css wiring import; and tests/clientStructure.test.js (palette, ES modules incl. node --check on ui.js, var() resolution, zero emojis). npm test 946/946 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 944), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

## Task 126 — main text contrast >= 17.05:1 (WCAG AAA)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-17

Starting fact (measured from first principles, WCAG 2.x relative luminance): the frozen --text #F4EFE6 on the frozen --bg #101114 is 16.48:1 — above the WCAG AAA floor (7:1) but BELOW the 17.05:1 project bar (--text on --surface is 13.82:1). Since the six palette values and their mapping are FROZEN (task 123) and new hex literals are banned, the only palette-compliant lever is a DERIVED page background via the sanctioned Layer-2 color-mix mechanism (exactly how --line/--surface-hi/--focus-ring are derived).

Deliverable: (1) tokens.css gained --bg-page: color-mix(in srgb, var(--bg) 65%, black) — the Archival Iron floor deepened by 35% black -> rgb(10,11,13), measured 17.19:1 against --text. The 70/30 mix was rejected: it clears the bar by only 0.04 (inside 8-bit rounding noise); 65/35 gives real margin. The six palette values, their tokens and the frozen task-123 mapping are UNTOUCHED — the derivation sits in Layer 2 like every other shade. (2) main.css: every TEXT-BEARING background now styles with var(--bg-page) — body, the input/select/button controls, and the .output-text reading surface; the frozen --bg keeps a real (non-text) consumer: the sandboxed iframe backdrop (which also removes the previous cream-coloured flash on iframe load, an incidental UX fix). The tokens.css header documents the measurement, the bar, and why the derivation exists.

Verification: NEW tests/contrast.test.js 4 gates, pure math, zero dependencies — (a) MEASURED RATIO: the effective 8-bit background colour (the same rounding a browser applies to the color-mix result) is computed from WCAG 2.x first principles and must clear 17.05:1 against the frozen --text (17.19:1 measured); (b) NECESSITY: the raw --text-on---bg pairing must measure BELOW the bar (16.48) while still clearing the AAA floor (7:1) — proving the derivation is required, not decorative, and the palette itself stays WCAG-legal; (c) TOKEN FORM: --bg-page is declared exactly as color-mix(in srgb, var(--bg) 65%, black) — references --bg, uses the `black` keyword, contains no hex literal; (d) WIRING: body, the controls rule and .output-text each background with var(--bg-page), no text-bearing rule keeps the raw --bg, and the iframe backdrop still consumes --bg (no dead token). Additionally tests/systemTokens.test.js now includes --bg-page in the Layer-2 discipline list (derived tokens may reference only the semantic layer). npm test 950/950 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 946), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.

Scope note: the server print document (HtmlPrintFormatter) keeps the canonical palette pairing (16.48:1 — far above the WCAG AAA floor); its stylesheet is pinned by task 104/123 parity and was not part of this client-side bar.

## Task 132 — status titlebar: name, logo, Qloo connectivity indicator

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-18

Deliverable: the top STATUS TITLEBAR in client/index.html (inside the banner landmark) — a .titlebar flex row: (1) the LOGO — an <img> of the real local asset ./public/favicon.svg at 28x28 with EXPLICIT width/height (no layout shift on load) and decorative alt="" (the h1 carries the name for assistive tech); (2) the NAME — <h1 class="brand">SUTO OSNOVA</h1> verbatim; (3) the CONNECTIVITY INDICATOR — #connectivity as a live region (role="status" aria-live="polite", data-state="checking") holding an aria-hidden state dot + the #connectivity-label span. New client/src/js/connectivity.js — the Qloo link monitor with FOUR HONEST STATES (the label TEXT changes per state, so the signal is never colour-only): checking (first probe pending), online (GET /api/health answered 200, status "ok" — Qloo reachable THROUGH the gateway), degraded (the BACKEND answered — HTTP 503, status "degraded" — the gateway is up, Qloo is not), offline (transport failure: the backend itself unreachable). stateFromProbe is a TOTAL pure function (garbage -> a safe state, never a throw; an HTTP answer even as 503 proves the backend is up; a payload WITHOUT an explicit ok status is NEVER claimed online); probeHealth NEVER rejects (wraps any failure into {ok:false,error}). The monitor: an immediate probe (node sits in "checking" until it answers), a 30s poll (no health-endpoint spam), a re-probe on window "online", writes via textContent/setAttribute only (XSS-safe), returns a stop() handle; import-safe in Node (no document/window touch at module load). api.js gained fetchHealth (the LOCAL /health route only). main.js starts the monitor on boot. CSS: .titlebar/.titlebar-logo/.connectivity/.connectivity-dot + state colours — gold dot = link live (8.93:1 on the floor), rust dot+label = degraded warning (5.64:1), muted = dead link (7.53:1) — all non-text/UI >= 3:1 and text >= AA on the page floor.

Verification: NEW tests/titlebar.test.js 5 gates — MARKUP (the h1 carries the EXACT name; the logo is the real local asset with explicit dimensions + decorative alt and the file exists; the indicator is a live region starting in checking with the aria-hidden dot and the label span); FOUR HONEST STATES (exact state set, four distinct labels); stateFromProbe TOTAL AND HONEST (online/degraded-503/degraded-missing-ok/transport-offline/garbage-null never throws); probeHealth NEVER REJECTS (success wraps payload, thrown errors wrap as {ok:false,error}); NO SPAM + XSS-SAFE + WIRED (POLL_INTERVAL_MS >= 10s, zero innerHTML (comments stripped before the probe — the contract prose mentions the sink by name), main.js calls startConnectivityMonitor(), fetchHealth targets /health only). Additionally tests/contrast.test.js gained a floor-pair gate: the degraded rust label on --bg-page clears WCAG AA (5.64:1). All prior client gates green (semantics, structure incl. the JS parse gate over the new module, tokens, typography). npm test 971/971 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 965), npm run lint clean. ## Task 136 — terminal progress indicator during graph polling

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-18

Deliverable: a DISCRETE MONOSPACE TERMINAL progress readout shown while a generate request polls the taste graph (new client/src/js/progress.js + the #progress element + main.js wiring + .progress CSS). FRAMES: a stepping ASCII bar plus a rotating spinner from the classic terminal set — `[####----] |` — advanced on a fixed 160ms interval: discrete frames, never a smooth animation, never a pictograph. PURE GENERATION: barFrame/spinnerFrame/progressFrame are pure and TOTAL (wrap-around for any tick, negatives included); the frames are ASCII-only (^[\[\]#\-|\\/ ]+$). A11Y CONTRACTS: (1) DECORATIVE MOTION — the readout lives OUTSIDE the aria-live status line with aria-hidden="true": screen readers hear the ONE status message ("Running worldbuilder mode..."), not a 160ms metronome (WCAG 4.1.3); (2) BOUNDED MOTION — the animation runs only while the request is in flight AND hard-caps at PROGRESS_MAX_DURATION_MS (30s) into a STATIC "still working" frame: moving content can never run forever (WCAG 2.2.2). LIFECYCLE: main.js starts it per submit and stops it in FINALLY — success and failure both clear every timer and EMPTY the element. ## Task 148 — Raw API Call Inspector modal (technical judges)

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-18

Deliverable: the Raw API Call Inspector — new client/src/js/inspector.js + a real dialog in index.html + a titlebar trigger + transport-edge recording in api.js + health/envelope feeders + main.js wiring + the modal CSS. THREE HONEST EVIDENCE SURFACES: (1) CLIENT CALL LOG — every request the client made to the LOCAL gateway (method, path, status, ok, ms), recorded by api.js at the transport edge (Date.now() timing; a transport FAILURE records status 0 too — the log shows what really happened) into a bounded FIFO store (createCallLog, entries frozen, copy-on-read); (2) QLOO CONNECTIVITY — the last SUCCESSFUL health probe's raw qloo + transport blocks (the connectivity monitor feeds noteHealth only on ok — a degraded/failed probe has no body to show, and the section says "not recorded yet"); (3) PROVENANCE LEDGER — the last dossier envelope's meta block (resolution/expansion/chain/... — the backend's own ledger of what Qloo was asked, fed by ui.renderJson). RAW VALUES, NEVER REWRITTEN: everything renders via textContent (JSON.stringify with indent / formatted call rows). MODAL CONTRACT: a real dialog (role=dialog, aria-modal, aria-labelledby, hidden at rest), Escape closes, the backdrop closes, focus moves to Close on open and RETURNS to the trigger on close; the note "Raw values, never rewritten" is pinned in the markup.

## Task 151 — hide default scrollbars, preserve scrolling

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-18

Deliverable: the reset block in client/src/css/main.css gained the two hiding declarations — `* { scrollbar-width: none; }` (the STANDARD property: Firefox and Chromium 121+) and `*::-webkit-scrollbar { display: none; }` (the WebKit/Blink pseudo-element for older engines). THE FUNCTIONALITY HALF IS THE CONTRACT: only the VISUAL bar disappears — every scroll CONTAINER keeps its overflow: auto/scroll unchanged (.output-text, .suggestions, .inspector-panel, .inspector-code), and each scrollable surface retains its bordered frame as the affordance that the hidden bar used to provide. The a11y rationale is documented in the rule's comment: the surfaces are few, bordered and visibly clipped, so scrollability stays discoverable without the bar.

Verification: tests/clientStructure.test.js gained a gate (13 tests in the file now): BOTH declarations must exist verbatim, AND the functionality-preservation probe asserts every pinned scroll container still declares overflow auto/scroll (a future "cleanup" that removed scrolling to hide the bar would fail). ## Task 153 — render performance: zero unnecessary DOM writes

Status: DONE
Date: 2026-10-18

Audit first: a pass over every client render path found FOUR real offenders, each now fixed and pinned. (1) THE GUARDS — new client/src/js/renderGuard.js: setTextIfChanged / setAttrIfChanged write ONLY when the value differs (the DOM diffs internally, but every redundant write still costs a style/layout invalidation round); a missing node is a silent false. (2) PROGRESS (the periodic path): the terminal readout rewrote its textContent every 160ms even when the frame string was identical (the bar+spinner cycle repeats every 44 ticks, and a restart/cap re-write was unconditional) — every write is now conditional. (3) CONNECTIVITY (the 30s poll): the indicator re-set data-state and the label text on EVERY probe even when nothing changed (live-region churn for screen readers too) — both writes are now guarded, and the monitor gained an injectable fetchImpl (the repo's injection discipline) so the unchanged-poll behaviour is testable. (4) ANCHOR INPUT (the per-keystroke path): typing rebuilt the whole listbox even when the match set was unchanged ("al" -> "alp" = zero DOM work via a match-id signature; a closed list invalidates the signature), and arrow navigation scanned ALL options with querySelectorAll and rewrote N aria-selected attributes per key — highlight is now TARGETED: it touches only the two affected options (the one leaving, the one entering). (5) STATUS: setStatus now guards both the text and the data-tone attribute (an identical repeat costs zero writes and zero live-region churn).

Verification: NEW tests/renderPerformance.test.js 6 gates — THE GUARDS (change writes once, a repeat writes zero, missing nodes are silent false); PROGRESS (an element already showing frame 0 costs ZERO writes on start; the cap frame writes once and never rewrites); CONNECTIVITY (several identical online polls produce at most the checking->online transition writes, then ZERO growth — text and attributes); ANCHOR INPUT (3 options render once; a narrowed set re-renders once; typing within the same set appends NOTHING; one ArrowDown writes ONE aria-selected, the second touches only the two affected options); STATUS (identical repeat = zero writes; changed message/tone = one write each); DISCIPLINE (all four hot-path modules import the shared guard; zero innerHTML). Fixed two fake-DOM gaps in the gate itself (a wrong premise about 'al' narrowing the match set; a missing removeEventListener on the input fake). npm test 1066/1066 (two consecutive stable runs; baseline 1060), npm run lint clean. Zero new dependencies.
